## S4-07 - evidence decision time at the scheduled cutoff: RETURN

Code reality (holds on main): `evidence/ledger_service.py:181` `decided_at=self.clock()` (and `:99`
`received_at = self.clock()`); `postgres_evidence_ledger.py:249-306` `decide_chunk`, `_coalesce_job` at `:376-404`
(widens only a PENDING overlapping job `FOR UPDATE`, else inserts one with `available_at = created_at = decided_at`);
`postgres_assessment_store.py:54` `d.decided_at <= :k`; `lease_job` `:192-222` uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and DB
`now`; `README-db.md:128-135` I3 residual and `:204` `S3_STALE_NO_JOB`; F-A test
`tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:385-424`. Rule 8: `recompute_job` is already in `$ledgerTables`
(`configuration-persistence.ps1:624-625`), so evidence-owned re-enqueue needs no ownership change; the packet correctly
forbids schedule-table writes.

### M1 - Medium - the mechanism, and with it the size and the proof path, is not decided

The packet leaves the mechanism open ("stamp ... at DB time ... or durably enqueue an additional job") and its
Architect question is conditional on a case it does not resolve. What it does not address is that `decided_at` is the
**injected test clock** across the deterministic real-Postgres fixtures:
- `BatchIngestionService(PostgresEvidenceLedger(URL), Clock(decided_at))` in `test_s3_dispatch_assessment_postgres.py:62`,
  `test_s4_finding_store_postgres.py:118` (the stepped `runner.tick(step["decided"])` used by the S4 Finding read,
  consequence store and evidence-history suites, with `decided` values such as `2026-10-30/31`, i.e. after today's DB
  time), `test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py` (`decided="2026-10-12..."`), `test_s2_site_evidence_postgres.py:276`
  (asserts the exact `decided_at`), and workers paired with `Clock(...)` cutoffs.
- If the ledger stamps `clock_timestamp()` unconditionally, every scenario whose worker cutoff is an injected past
  instant excludes all evidence, and future-dated `decided` steps lose their ordering. That is a cross-suite rewrite,
  not an S slice.
- If instead the injected clock is kept as an override and only the production composition (`main.py:267`, no clock)
  stamps DB time, then the existing fixtures exercise a path production does not use, Build 2's "deliberately fast
  and slow app clock" has no effect on the fixed path, and the existing F-A alarm test (which injects
  `decided = db_now + 20 s`) keeps passing only through the override. The packet must say which path the race and
  restart proofs use and how the alarm stays tested.
- `_coalesce_job` sets `available_at`/`created_at` from the same `decided_at`; with an app clock ahead, a newly
  inserted job is not leasable until DB time passes it (`available_at <= :now`). The packet does not say whether those
  move to DB time too.

Why it matters: the brief asked whether the conditional mechanism is decided enough to build. It is not: the
implementer would have to choose a test-clock contract with cross-suite consequences, and the choice changes the
size and what the acceptance proof actually proves.

Fix (Planner fix round; an Architect ruling only if the Planner keeps an injectable `decided_at` in production):
1. Decide the mechanism. Reviewer note for that decision (to be proved in build, not adopted): with `decided_at`
   from `clock_timestamp()` inside the chunk transaction, the existing coalesce appears sufficient. If the coalesce
   `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` widens a PENDING job, `lease_job`'s `SKIP LOCKED` cannot take that job until the
   ingestion commit, so its DB cutoff is later than the decision and the row is visible. If the worker leased first,
   the coalesce sees LEASED (after EvalPlanQual if it was blocked) and inserts a new PENDING job. If that holds, the
   packet's Architect question is moot, and "DB-time stamp, existing coalesce, no new job path" can be stated as the
   design with the race test as its proof.
2. State the clock contract: for example, the ledger takes `decided_at` (and job `available_at`/`created_at`) from
   the DB inside the transaction, with an explicit, named test-only override for deterministic fixtures. Say that the
   race/restart proofs run on the production (DB) path, list the suites that keep the override, and replace the
   F-A alarm test's driver with the override (the alarm stays as defense in depth).
3. Re-size against that: either confirm S with the override, or split if fixtures must migrate.

Lows (carry):
- L1: state whether `ingestion_batch.received_at`/`completed_at` stay on the app clock ("preserve receipt times" reads
  as yes); the receipt API exposes both.
- L2: the I3 residual also mentions configuration `recorded_at` vs `reconcile` (phase-2 review residual risk).
  Packet should state it stays out of scope (it self-heals) so the closeout does not over-claim.

## Code reality and boundary

- `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:174-184` passes `self.clock()` to each chunk before the ledger transaction. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:249-306` owns the transaction, writes accepted rows/dispositions and coalesces a recompute job; this is the writer to change.
- `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:50-54` selects ACCEPTED dispositions only when `d.decided_at <= :k`. Scheduled stages use DB time; the cutover read and F-A reproduction are in `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:386` onward. `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204` documents the residual/alarm/requeue.
- Rule 8 keeps the evidence ledger writer on its tables and the assessment writer on S3 jobs (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:680-757`). Keep those paths and table ownership; do not add schedule-table writes to evidence code. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).

## Build and acceptance

1. Choose an evidence-owned transactional fix: stamp each newly decided chunk at DB time inside its write transaction and ensure coalescing cannot lose a decision whose commit occurs after a worker's cutoff/read, or durably enqueue an additional job for that case. Use a committed decision's DB-visible order as the invariant; simply changing the Python clock source before the transaction is insufficient. Preserve receipt times, duplicate/rejected semantics, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry.
2. Real-Postgres race proof holds a pending/leased S3 job and a scheduled worker at its DB cutoff, then commits an overlapping late evidence chunk with a deliberately fast and slow app clock. The next tick must either include that accepted ID in the run at a cutoff at or after its decision, or find durable live work that makes the window non-CURRENT until a later run includes it. Assert the disposition time, job state, run cutoff, selection sequence and read status; no `STALE_NO_JOB` terminal gap.
3. Crash/restart after the evidence transaction and before the next tick; replay the same batch and run ticks. Assert one accepted record, no lost job, eventual current assessment citing the ID, and no falsely CURRENT intermediate read. Check a chunk spanning two default windows, a duplicate-only chunk, and a different Site. Keep the documented alarm as defense in depth and update `backend/README-db.md` to state the remaining recovery condition accurately.

Focused checks: `backend/tests/test_s2_evidence_ledger_postgres.py`, `test_s3_dispatch_assessment_postgres.py`, `test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py`, plus the new race/restart case and `tools/check-all.ps1`. On final revision run one real-Postgres full backend suite per `backend/TESTING.md` with JUnit zero skipped/failed, keep-awake and 20-minute cap. Give the reviewer same-revision evidence. No UI checkpoint.

## Architect questions

If a transactionally safe DB-time stamp still permits a worker snapshot to miss a later commit whose timestamp precedes its cutoff, recommend evidence-owned durable re-enqueue through the existing recompute-job contract, with any assessment-writer API change explicit in review. The invariant above is mandatory whichever mechanism is chosen; no new schedule-table writer is authorized.

190:    # --- jobs -------------------------------------------------------------------

192:    def lease_job(self, domain: str, now: datetime, lease: timedelta, *,
193:                  max_attempts: int = 3, tenant_id: UUID | None = None,
194:                  site_id: UUID | None = None) -> JobLease | None:
195:        """Lease the oldest due job of `domain`; with `tenant_id` and `site_id`, only that Site's jobs (S4-02d:
196:        the schedule leases per Site, which the global FIFO cannot make fair). Both the expire-to-FAILED step and
197:        the lease are scoped the same way."""
198:        if (tenant_id is None) != (site_id is None):
199:            raise ValueError("A Site-scoped lease names both the tenant and the Site.")
200:        scope = "" if site_id is None else " AND tenant_id = :t AND site_id = :s"
201:        scoped = {} if site_id is None else {"t": tenant_id, "s": site_id}
202:
203:        def work(c: Connection) -> JobLease | None:
204:            # A lease that expired on its last allowed attempt (the worker crashed or always outlives
205:            # its lease) is FAILED with a reason instead of being re-leased forever (phase-2 F6).
206:            c.execute(text(
207:                "UPDATE recompute_job SET status = 'FAILED', lease_until = NULL, last_error = "
208:                "'lease expired on attempt ' || attempts || ' of ' || CAST(:max AS integer) || "
209:                "' without completing' WHERE domain = :d AND status = 'LEASED' AND lease_until < :now "
210:                "AND attempts >= :max" + scope), {"d": domain, "now": now, "max": max_attempts, **scoped})
211:            row = c.execute(text(
212:                "UPDATE recompute_job SET status = 'LEASED', lease_until = :until, attempts = attempts + 1 "
213:                "WHERE id = (SELECT id FROM recompute_job WHERE domain = :d AND ((status = 'PENDING' AND "
214:                "available_at <= :now) OR (status = 'LEASED' AND lease_until < :now))" + scope +
215:                " ORDER BY available_at, created_at LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) "
216:                "RETURNING id, tenant_id, site_id, domain, window_start, window_end, attempts"),
217:                {"d": domain, "now": now, "until": now + lease, **scoped}).first()
218:            if row is None:
219:                return None
220:            return JobLease(row[0], row[1], row[2], row[3], Window(row[4], row[5]), row[6], now)
221:
222:        return self._run(work, write=True)
223:
224:    def _still_mine(self, c: Connection, lease: JobLease) -> None:
225:        row = c.execute(text("SELECT status, attempts FROM recompute_job WHERE id = :id FOR UPDATE"),
226:                        {"id": lease.job_id}).first()

FILE backend/README-db.md
117-- **Output:** one JSON line per processed item, then a summary line with the Site scope and the `selected`,
118-  `appended`, `replayed`, `no_op`, `failed`, `deferred` and `backlog` counts. Exit 1 when any item failed.
119:- S3 `process-jobs` and S4 `match-findings` are not run by this CLI; the S4-02d tick runs all three stages.
120-
121-## Analytic and Finding worker schedule (S4-02d)
122-
123:One environment-owned tick runs, per selected Site: S3 `process-jobs` (Site-scoped), S4 `match-findings` only
124-when the matcher fence or version moved, then the consequence drain, each in a killable child with server-side
125-`statement_timeout`/`lock_timeout`. Claims, leases, back-off, heartbeat and every health threshold use database
126-time (`clock_timestamp()`); the scheduled S3 worker, matcher and drain receive DB time as their `now`.
127-
128:**I3 residual (open, evidence/ingestion owner).** The scheduled S3 knowledge cutoff is DB time, but
129:`ingestion_disposition.decided_at` is still the ingestion host's application clock. If that host runs ahead of
130:the database, a reading decided after the cutoff of a PENDING job it widened is left out of the run; the job
131-completes and the window reads `STALE_PROJECTION` (`INPUT_BASIS_CHANGED`) with no live job. The seam never reads
132:it CURRENT, and health raises `S3_STALE_NO_JOB` (Site `FAILED`) instead of `IDLE`; recover with `requeue --day`
133:once the DB clock has passed the decision. The fix (DB-time `decided_at`, or a new job when a widened job's lease
134-could predate the decision) belongs to a separate evidence-owner packet; this schedule does not change ingestion.
135-
--
202-| `MATCH_EVIDENCE_NOT_ACCEPTED` | Deterministic matcher defect: a cited evidence ID is not accepted in this Site. | Code/data defect; not retried into success. |
203-| `S3_JOB_FAILED` | A terminal FAILED S3 job (ID, window, `last_error`); `reconcile` will not re-enqueue it. | After fixing the cause: `python -m assetops_backend.analytics.assess_dispatch requeue --tenant-id T --site-id S --job-id J`; the new DEFAULT selection supersedes the failure. `assess` is not recovery. |
204:| `S3_STALE_NO_JOB` | A selected UTC day reads `STALE_PROJECTION` (for example `INPUT_BASIS_CHANGED`) and no live S3 job intersects it, so no tick will rebuild it (I3 residual above). | Once the DB clock has passed the late decision: `python -m assetops_backend.analytics.assess_dispatch requeue --tenant-id T --site-id S --day YYYY-MM-DD` (refused unless that day is STALE with no live job); the next tick rebuilds it. |
205-| `CONSEQUENCE_ATTEMPTS_GROWING` | Unsucceeded consequence work with 5+ attempts (no terminal cap; ~90/day). | Repair the cause; `recompute_consequences pending` lists the rows. |
206-| `STALE_NO_WORK` | A pre-0012 consequence head is STALE with no recorded work (Finding/revision IDs given). | Reported only; not backfilled by this slice. |
--
208-| `CONSEQUENCE_CLAIMED` (stage code) | The drain's Site lock is held elsewhere, usually a legacy per-Site timer. | Retire the per-Site timer. |
209-
210:**Manual Leg B commands:** `process-jobs`, `match-findings` and `recompute_consequences run` do not take the
211-schedule's Site claim. **Stop the tick (disable the timer) and confirm `heads` shows no Site claim before running
212-any of them by hand**, then re-enable it. The matcher fence still protects Finding writes, but S3/S4 serialisation

168:    # --- internals ----------------------------------------------------------
169:
170:    def _drive(self, handle: BatchHandle, envelopes: tuple[str, ...], basis) -> None:
171:        status = handle.status
172:        chunks = 0
173:        while status != "COMPLETED":
174:            if self.max_chunks_per_request is not None and chunks >= self.max_chunks_per_request:
175:                return
176:            status = self.ledger.decide_chunk(
177:                handle,
178:                envelopes,
179:                lambda ordinal, text: decide(basis, ordinal, text),
180:                limit=self.chunk_size,
181:                decided_at=self.clock(),
182:            )
183:            chunks += 1
184:            if self.crash_after_chunks is not None and chunks >= self.crash_after_chunks and status != "COMPLETED":
185:                raise SimulatedCrash(f"crashed after {chunks} committed chunk(s)")

# Backend testing guide

How to run the backend suite, when to run all of it, and how to prove a full
run was real. Database setup itself is in [README-db.md](README-db.md).

## When to run what

- **While building a phase:** run the focused files for the area you changed,
  plus adjacent integration tests. When an interface crosses into the host or
  gateway, also run those suites.
- **At the end of an ordinary slice:** one real-Postgres full backend suite,
  on the slice's final revision, with the JUnit zero-skip check below. Cite it
  in the review packet.
- **Reviewers** run the focused files relevant to their findings and cite the
  implementer's same-revision full evidence. A reviewer reruns the full suite
  only for a finding that needs it.
- **One full suite at a time on this machine.** Concurrent full runs were not
  attempted and are not proven safe; a full run is disk-bound, so a second one
  (or another Postgres-heavy agent) slows both and makes timing meaningless.
- OPS-01's baseline and comparison runs are the one documented exception to
  "one full suite per slice".
- `tools/check-all.ps1` is a separate required guard command; it does not run
  pytest, and pytest does not replace it.

## The test database

`ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` names a base database whose name ends in `_test`
(Compose creates `assetops_test`; agents may use a private one such as
`assetops_<task>_test`). Without it every Postgres module is reported as
**skipped**, and such a run is not valid full-suite proof.

The base database is used only as an administrative connection. At
configuration time `tests/conftest.py` calls `pg_harness.route()`, which
rewrites `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` to a run-owned name before any test module
is imported:

```
<base without _test>_pt<run>_<tag>_test     tag = main (serial), gw0, gw1... (xdist workers), tpl (template)
```

On first use the harness migrates the template to head once, then every
Postgres module's `engine` fixture drops the run's worker database `WITH
(FORCE)` and clones it from the template (`CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE`), and
asserts the clone is at the Alembic head. Each module therefore still starts on
a fresh, fully migrated schema; migration up/downgrade tests still run in place
on that module's clone. `store` fixtures yield and dispose their engine.

Between tests the S1 `store` fixture empties the same tables
`TRUNCATE foundation_revision, source, gateway, asset, site_import, site, tenant
CASCADE` would, but with `DELETE` under `session_replication_role = replica`
(`pg_harness.empty_tables`). TRUNCATE rebuilds every index and on PostgreSQL 16
each rebuild forces a data-file sync, which Docker Desktop makes slow and
erratic (measured 1.4 s quiet, 22-25 s under disk pressure, per test; DELETE
0.14 s). `test_pg_harness.py` proves the DELETE set equals the tables TRUNCATE
CASCADE reports. `ASSETOPS_TEST_CLEANUP=truncate` restores TRUNCATE.

- In parallel (`-n 2`) the xdist controller runs no tests. It makes the run
  id and passes it to each worker; each worker routes itself to
  `..._pt<run>_gw<N>_test` before it imports a test module. The first worker
  that needs the template builds it under an advisory lock and the others reuse
  it. Workers drop only their own database; the controller drops the template
  and anything else the run left (a crashed worker's database) at the end.
  Under `--dist loadfile` every module stays on one worker, so module-scoped
  fixtures behave as serially. With `--no-loadscope-reorder` modules are handed
  out in collection order, and workers move the two long S4 Finding modules
  (`conftest.START_FIRST`) to the front so they start first; serial order is
  unchanged.
- Parametrize IDs must not be random: xdist refuses to run when workers collect
  different node IDs (`Different tests were collected between gw0 and gw1`).
- Nothing is provisioned for `--collect-only`, for a selection without Postgres

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-38-
--
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-41-Adapters poll or subscribe to Modbus/serial, meters, PLC/BMS and vendor HTTP/OPC-UA interfaces as actually encountered at a pilot; each yields a typed device report with source clock, unit, quality and device identity. Site-local, versioned mapping binds `(device, signal)` to a canonical signal/semantic role, applies documented unit conversion, and rejects unknown or incompatible readings. The gateway generates `SourceEnvelope` after mapping, with `source_time`, `published_at`, quality/unavailable reason, interval bounds where relevant, gateway/source/device/signal IDs, mapping version and a stable message identity. Backend sets `received_at`; the gateway cannot impersonate it. Clock skew is recorded/flagged, not silently corrected. An explicit site timezone is presentation context; instants are UTC.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-42-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:43:SQLite in WAL mode contains gateway identity/configuration, mapping snapshots, local monotonic sequence allocation per stable source stream, append-only envelope outbox, upload batches/acknowledgements and compact health counters. Write envelope and sequence in one transaction before upload. Preserve bytes and IDs across restarts, backoff with jitter, retry identical batch bytes, and delete/compact only after the server confirms each envelope's accepted/duplicate/rejected/conflict outcome and the acknowledgement is durable locally. Rejected/conflicting messages move to an inspectable dead-letter state; a whole-batch refusal retains the batch for operator repair. Ordering is by source time plus sequence for analysis, never by arrival order; late records trigger recomputation of affected periods. A sequence gap is a source-health signal, not fabricated telemetry. Expose last device contact, queue age/depth, disk pressure, clock offset, last successful upload and mapping errors locally and centrally. Limit disk use with alerts and explicit retention policy; do not silently drop unacknowledged evidence.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-44-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-45-**Contract move required:** current `SourceEnvelope` derives `message_id` from site/gateway/source/device/signal/time and has `sequence` outside that identity (`contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope.py`). Two legitimate samples at the same timestamp can collide. Introduce an additive new envelope version whose identity includes stable source event ID or persisted sequence; retain parsers for historic v1/v2 records and reject identity reuse with different content. Define sequence scope, reset/epoch and batch acknowledgement schema before a real pilot. Never regenerate IDs on retry. Gateway “intelligence” stops at acquisition, mapping, validation, buffering and diagnostics; cross-device inference, Findings, economic models and verification stay central.

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-47-### Ingestion and transport
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-48-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:49:Use `POST /api/ingestion/batches` over TLS with gateway credentials scoped to site/source, bounded batch size, rate limits and a queryable batch receipt. Authenticate the declared site and gateway against registered identity; do not trust `source_mode` or mapping/version references merely because they are present in JSON. Resolve the mapping and Foundation version effective **at source time**, including old versions after edits. Validate schema, clocks, units, role binding and provenance, then atomically persist acceptance decision, raw envelope digest/reference, normalized accepted record or rejection/conflict, and batch progress in Postgres. Unique keys for `(site, gateway, source, message_id)` and `(site, batch_id)` make retries idempotent; same key and different bytes is a conflict, not overwrite. Return per-envelope disposition and a receipt only after commit. Do not let a malformed sibling hide valid records. An outbox/work table in the same transaction schedules analytic recomputation; a worker leases jobs, retries safely and publishes revisioned read models. The UI exposes ingestion lag so “no finding yet” is not interpreted as healthy.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-50-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-51-Direct HTTPS is sufficient while pilots have tens of sites, bounded upload volume, and workers can keep up with Postgres. Upgrade to MQTT when device/site topology or near-real-time command/control requires brokered sessions; add a managed queue between receipt and workers when sustained ingest spikes or independent consumer fan-out exceed Postgres job capacity; adopt streaming infrastructure only when measured throughput/latency/replay demands it. These change transport or processing, not the envelope or acceptance semantics.
--
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-55-Keep explicit relational entities: `Tenant/Site`, `Asset` (stable physical identity), `FoundationRevision` (valid-time, recorded-time, topology, objectives, controls), `SignalBindingRevision`, `Gateway/Source`, `IngestionBatch`, `AcceptedEvidence`, `EvidenceRejection`, `AnalyticRun/AssessmentRevision`, `Finding` + immutable `FindingRevision`, `FinancialAssumptionRevision`, `FinancialConsequenceRevision`, `Action/ActionEvent`, `VerificationPlanRevision/Outcome`. Evidence retains raw digest, original and normalized value/unit, timestamps, quality, mapping/configuration revision, and provenance. No retroactive rewrite of a past basis: backdated corrections create a new revision and re-evaluation with links to both versions.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-56-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:57:Analytics are pure, versioned functions of accepted evidence, time-valid public configuration and declared assumption versions. Each output records evidence IDs/window, model version, claim level, gaps/alternatives and input digest. A Finding has stable identity and separate lifecycle events; changed evidence creates a revision, not a new indistinguishable row. A consequence names technical driver, quantity, formula, currency, assumption revision, measured/modelled/extrapolated/scenario basis and overlap group. Never add incompatible currencies, windows or overlapping causes. Verification compares a later accepted window with the plan's target **and** service/asset guardrails; return Verified, Ineffective or Inconclusive, with later recurrence able to reopen the Finding.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-58-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:59:Use Postgres partitions/indexes on `(site_id, source_time)` if real evidence volume warrants them; ordinary indexed tables are enough initially. Read projections can be Postgres tables/materialized views or bounded query services. Cache only reproducible, keyed views by site/window/domain/revision and invalidate on late evidence, changed configuration or analytic version. The database is the source of truth; a cache is never a second authority.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-60-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-61-### Simulator and real-site equivalence
--
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-67-| Gateway | Protocol adapters, mappings, SQLite outbox, HTTPS | Same gateway contract and SQLite outbox; virtual device adapter and faultable link |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-68-| Crossing | `SourceEnvelope` batch | Same `SourceEnvelope` batch, `SIMULATED` provenance |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:69:| Product | Shared ingestion → Postgres → analytics → APIs → UI | Exactly the same |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-70-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:71:The world owns physics, controls, scenario causes and oracle expectations; simulated devices own only what their real counterparts could report. The simulated gateway cannot read private world state except through device reports. Its SQLite store persists staged envelopes and upload state exactly as a physical gateway would. Preserve Lab Draft/Stage/Commit only as a **release gate**: Draft may execute and inspect private truth; Stage seals public envelopes; Commit authorizes delivery through the same batch API and records a manifest/receipt. Commit cannot write evidence, Findings or site projections. Private truth, controller intent, causes, oracle and run controls live in a separate Lab store and Lab-only API with access controls; no foreign key or import from normal analytics to those objects. Simulated provenance may label a claim, never alter its calculation.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-72-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-73-### Domain seam and screen reads
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-74-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:75:The shared shell owns identity, site/window selection, evidence drawer, claim status, Findings, money and work navigation. A small registry keyed by `site_type` selects explicit evidence roles, analytic functions, summary sections and labels. A role binding maps physical signals to analytic inputs for a *valid period*; it is not a runtime plugin. Missing roles lower or block claim capability. Mini-grid roles include generator state/power, feeder demand, PV availability, battery reserve/SOC, fuel and service baseline; cold-chain roles include chamber temperature, setpoint/policy, door/defrost, compressor and backup state; EV roles include session request/energy/end state, connector availability, charger limits and tariff. Keep roles minimal per analytic, not one global schema that pretends domains are interchangeable.

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-79-| Portfolio `GET /portfolio` | Site assessment coverage, ranked Finding index, consequence rollup, work/verification summaries | Priorities and consequence categories retain domain and currency; no opaque score. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-80-| Site Overview `GET /sites/{id}/overview` | Site objective cards, bounded metrics, timeline, latest Finding/consequence/action, source freshness | Mini-grid service/dispatch; cold temperature/backup; EV sessions/availability. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:81:| Performance `GET /sites/{id}/performance/{view}` | Windowed analytic revisions plus evidence coverage | Dispatch/service/fuel; temperature/cooling/backup; sessions/availability/energy. |

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-84-| Financials `GET /sites/{id}/financials`, `/financials` | Consequence revisions, assumption versions and compatible rollups | Fuel/maintenance, product/reliability exposure, EV revenue/service loss; show unpriced risk. |

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-87-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:88:These are API families, not a command to build every mock screen immediately. Site-scoped lists filter the **same** global Finding/Action objects. Routes without a supported read model stay out of navigation; supported routes with missing evidence show a reason and next acquisition step. Example claim path: generator state/power and PV/battery/load envelopes → accepted records tied to mapping/Foundation revisions → versioned dispatch interval classification → Finding revision + bounded fuel/maintenance consequence under assumption revision → Site/Portfolio projection → Finding card and evidence drawer. The drawer resolves exact IDs and original envelope bytes; it never reconstructs lineage from a card label.

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:95:| Central Postgres | Site/asset/configuration/assumptions, acceptance ledger, normalized evidence, analytic and Finding revisions, work and read projections | Transactional multi-site source of product truth. |

-## Build and acceptance
-
-1. Choose an evidence-owned transactional fix: stamp each newly decided chunk at DB time inside its write transaction and ensure coalescing cannot lose a decision whose commit occurs after a worker's cutoff/read, or durably enqueue an additional job for that case. Preserve receipt times, duplicate/rejected semantics, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry.
-2. Real-Postgres race proof holds a pending/leased S3 job and a scheduled worker at its DB cutoff, then commits an overlapping late evidence chunk with a deliberately fast and slow app clock. The next tick must either include that accepted ID in the run at a cutoff at or after its decision, or find durable live work that makes the window non-CURRENT until a later run includes it. Assert the disposition time, job state, run cutoff, selection sequence and read status; no `STALE_NO_JOB` terminal gap.
-3. Crash/restart after the evidence transaction and before the next tick; replay the same batch and run ticks. Assert one accepted record, no lost job, eventual current assessment citing the ID, and no falsely CURRENT intermediate read. Check a chunk spanning two default windows, a duplicate-only chunk, and a different Site. +## Decided clock contract
 
-## Code reality and boundary
+- Production `PostgresEvidenceLedger.decide_chunk` obtains one `clock_timestamp()` from the **database inside the chunk write transaction**, after locking the batch row and before writing its dispositions. Use this one instant for every newly decided ordinal's `ingestion_disposition.decided_at` in the chunk. Do not use `now()`/`transaction_timestamp()` (which can predate a lock wait), the ingestion host's clock, or a timestamp sampled before the transaction. The same decision instant supplies `recompute_job.available_at` and `created_at` **when `_coalesce_job` inserts a new job**. A widened PENDING job retains its existing timestamps. These writes commit or roll back together. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:176-182` currently passes the app clock; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:251-307,376-404` owns the transactional change.
+- Give the ledger an explicitly named `test_decision_clock_override` (callable, or equally explicit test-only fixture seam). When absent, always use the DB-time path; production composition at `backend/assetops_backend/main.py:265-269` must never set it. Deterministic fixtures set the override on the ledger, with one override result per chunk for both `decided_at` and a newly inserted job's `available_at`/`created_at`. The override is for fixture ordering and F-A alarm coverage, never evidence for the production race or restart proof. Keep `BatchIngestionService`'s app clock for `ingestion_batch.received_at` and `completed_at` (receipt fields); pass completion time separately from decision time so a DB-stamped decision does not silently change receipt semantics.
+- Preserve duplicate/rejected outcomes, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry. Only newly processed ordinals receive a new decision instant; a retry of a committed chunk retains its stored dispositions. Configuration `recorded_at` versus `reconcile` is a separate, self-healing comparand and is outside this packet.
 
-- `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:174-184` passes `self.clock()` to each chunk before the ledger transaction. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:249-306` owns the transaction, writes accepted rows/dispositions and coalesces a recompute job; this is the writer to change.
-- `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:50-54` selects ACCEPTED dispositions only when `d.decided_at <= :k`. Scheduled stages use DB time; the cutover read and F-A reproduction are in `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:386` onward. `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204` documents the residual/alarm/requeue.
-- Rule 8 keeps the evidence ledger writer on its tables and the assessment writer on S3 jobs (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:680-757`). Keep those paths and table ownership; do not add schedule-table writes to evidence code. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).
+## Why existing coalescing should suffice - proof obligation
+
+`_coalesce_job` locks an overlapping PENDING job with `FOR UPDATE` and widens it inside the ingestion transaction (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:376-404`). The worker lease uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:203-222`), and selection admits accepted evidence only at `d.decided_at <= :k` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:47-56`). Prove both interleavings in real Postgres: if ingestion locks/widens first, the worker cannot lease that row until ingestion commits; its later DB cutoff can see the decision. If the worker leases first, ingestion cannot widen that leased row and inserts a new PENDING job, due at the DB decision time, for the next tick. Include the blocked/rechecked status case, and prove the outcome when a tick passes while a new job is still uncommitted. The required invariant is that no committed accepted decision is excluded by a finished run with no live work. If either interleaving fails, add an evidence-owned transactional re-enqueue through `recompute_job`; rule 8 already lists it among the ledger writer's tables (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:622-625`). Do not add schedule-table writes or silently expand assessment-writer ownership.
 
 ## Build and acceptance
 
-1. Choose an evidence-owned transactional fix: stamp each newly decided chunk at DB time inside its write transaction and ensure coalescing cannot lose a decision whose commit occurs after a worker's cutoff/read, or durably enqueue an additional job for that case. Use a committed decision's DB-visible order as the invariant; simply changing the Python clock source before the transaction is insufficient. Preserve receipt times, duplicate/rejected semantics, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry.
-2. Real-Postgres race proof holds a pending/leased S3 job and a scheduled worker at its DB cutoff, then commits an overlapping late evidence chunk with a deliberately fast and slow app clock. The next tick must either include that accepted ID in the run at a cutoff at or after its decision, or find durable live work that makes the window non-CURRENT until a later run includes it. Assert the disposition time, job state, run cutoff, selection sequence and read status; no `STALE_NO_JOB` terminal gap.
-3. Crash/restart after the evidence transaction and before the next tick; replay the same batch and run ticks. Assert one accepted record, no lost job, eventual current assessment citing the ID, and no falsely CURRENT intermediate read. Check a chunk spanning two default windows, a duplicate-only chunk, and a different Site. Keep the documented alarm as defense in depth and update `backend/README-db.md` to state the remaining recovery condition accurately.

Hold an overlapping PENDING job and scheduled worker around its DB cutoff; commit a late chunk under deliberately fast and slow `BatchIngestionService` app clocks. Exercise ingestion-first, worker-first and worker-skips-uncommitted-job orders. Assert the DB-sourced disposition instant and inserted job timestamps, job lock/status/window, run cutoff, selected accepted ID sequence and read state. At a cutoff at or after the decision, the run must cite that ID; otherwise durable live work must keep the window non-CURRENT until a later tick includes it. No terminal `S3_STALE_NO_JOB` gap.

Separately re-drive the existing F-A alarm test at `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:385-424` **through `test_decision_clock_override`**, deliberately setting `decided_at` ahead of DB time; retain its stale/no-job alarm and requeue assertions as defense-in-depth coverage, and label it as an override-path test.
+4. Update `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204,210-212` and the I3 backlog closeout accurately. Scheduled ticks use DB time for this comparand. Manual app-clock `process-jobs` run beside the tick can still mix clocks; I3 is **not** claimed closed for manual CLIs. Retain the runbook rule to stop the tick before manual Leg B commands. Configuration `recorded_at` remains outside the closeout. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).

+## Decided clock contract
 
-## Code reality and boundary
+- Production `PostgresEvidenceLedger.decide_chunk` obtains one `clock_timestamp()` from the **database inside the chunk write transaction**, after locking the batch row and before writing its dispositions. Use this one instant for every newly decided ordinal's `ingestion_disposition.decided_at` in the chunk. Do not use `now()`/`transaction_timestamp()` (which can predate a lock wait), the ingestion host's clock, or a timestamp sampled before the transaction. The same decision instant supplies `recompute_job.available_at` and `created_at` **when `_coalesce_job` inserts a new job**. A widened PENDING job retains its existing timestamps. These writes commit or roll back together. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:176-182` currently passes the app clock; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:251-307,376-404` owns the transactional change.
+- Give the ledger an explicitly named `test_decision_clock_override` (callable, or equally explicit test-only fixture seam). When absent, always use the DB-time path; production composition at `backend/assetops_backend/main.py:265-269` must never set it. Deterministic fixtures set the override on the ledger, with one override result per chunk for both `decided_at` and a newly inserted job's `available_at`/`created_at`. The override is for fixture ordering and F-A alarm coverage, never evidence for the production race or restart proof. Keep `BatchIngestionService`'s app clock for `ingestion_batch.received_at` and `completed_at` (receipt fields); pass completion time separately from decision time so a DB-stamped decision does not silently change receipt semantics.
+- Preserve duplicate/rejected outcomes, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry. Only newly processed ordinals receive a new decision instant; a retry of a committed chunk retains its stored dispositions. Configuration `recorded_at` versus `reconcile` is a separate, self-healing comparand and is outside this packet.
 
-- `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:174-184` passes `self.clock()` to each chunk before the ledger transaction. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:249-306` owns the transaction, writes accepted rows/dispositions and coalesces a recompute job; this is the writer to change.
-- `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:50-54` selects ACCEPTED dispositions only when `d.decided_at <= :k`. Scheduled stages use DB time; the cutover read and F-A reproduction are in `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:386` onward. `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204` documents the residual/alarm/requeue.
-- Rule 8 keeps the evidence ledger writer on its tables and the assessment writer on S3 jobs (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:680-757`). Keep those paths and table ownership; do not add schedule-table writes to evidence code. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).
+## Why existing coalescing should suffice - proof obligation
+
+`_coalesce_job` locks an overlapping PENDING job with `FOR UPDATE` and widens it inside the ingestion transaction (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:376-404`). The worker lease uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:203-222`), and selection admits accepted evidence only at `d.decided_at <= :k` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:47-56`). Prove both interleavings in real Postgres: if ingestion locks/widens first, the worker cannot lease that row until ingestion commits; its later DB cutoff can see the decision. If the worker leases first, ingestion cannot widen that leased row and inserts a new PENDING job, due at the DB decision time, for the next tick. Include the blocked/rechecked status case, and prove the outcome when a tick passes while a new job is still uncommitted. The required invariant is that no committed accepted decision is excluded by a finished run with no live work. If either interleaving fails, add an evidence-owned transactional re-enqueue through `recompute_job`; rule 8 already lists it among the ledger writer's tables (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:622-625`). Do not add schedule-table writes or silently expand assessment-writer ownership.

-## Code reality and boundary
+## Decided clock contract
 
-- `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:174-184` passes `self.clock()` to each chunk before the ledger transaction. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:249-306` owns the transaction, writes accepted rows/dispositions and coalesces a recompute job; this is the writer to change.
-- `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:50-54` selects ACCEPTED dispositions only when `d.decided_at <= :k`. Scheduled stages use DB time; the cutover read and F-A reproduction are in `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:386` onward. `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204` documents the residual/alarm/requeue.
-- Rule 8 keeps the evidence ledger writer on its tables and the assessment writer on S3 jobs (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:680-757`). Keep those paths and table ownership; do not add schedule-table writes to evidence code. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).
+- Production `PostgresEvidenceLedger.decide_chunk` obtains one `clock_timestamp()` from the **database inside the chunk write transaction**, after locking the batch row and before writing its dispositions. Use this one instant for every newly decided ordinal's `ingestion_disposition.decided_at` in the chunk. Do not use `now()`/`transaction_timestamp()` (which can predate a lock wait), the ingestion host's clock, or a timestamp sampled before the transaction. The same decision instant supplies `recompute_job.available_at` and `created_at` **when `_coalesce_job` inserts a new job**. A widened PENDING job retains its existing timestamps. These writes commit or roll back together. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:176-182` currently passes the app clock; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:251-307,376-404` owns the transactional change.
+- Give the ledger an explicitly named `test_decision_clock_override` (callable, or equally explicit test-only fixture seam). When absent, always use the DB-time path; production composition at `backend/assetops_backend/main.py:265-269` must never set it. Deterministic fixtures set the override on the ledger, with one override result per chunk for both `decided_at` and a newly inserted job's `available_at`/`created_at`. The override is for fixture ordering and F-A alarm coverage, never evidence for the production race or restart proof. Keep `BatchIngestionService`'s app clock for `ingestion_batch.received_at` and `completed_at` (receipt fields); pass completion time separately from decision time so a DB-stamped decision does not silently change receipt semantics.
+- Preserve duplicate/rejected outcomes, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry. Only newly processed ordinals receive a new decision instant; a retry of a committed chunk retains its stored dispositions. Configuration `recorded_at` versus `reconcile` is a separate, self-healing comparand and is outside this packet.
+
+## Why existing coalescing should suffice - proof obligation
+
+`_coalesce_job` locks an overlapping PENDING job with `FOR UPDATE` and widens it inside the ingestion transaction (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:376-404`). The worker lease uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:203-222`), and selection admits accepted evidence only at `d.decided_at <= :k` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:47-56`). Prove both interleavings in real Postgres: if ingestion locks/widens first, the worker cannot lease that row until ingestion commits; its later DB cutoff can see the decision. If the worker leases first, ingestion cannot widen that leased row and inserts a new PENDING job, due at the DB decision time, for the next tick. Include the blocked/rechecked status case, and prove the outcome when a tick passes while a new job is still uncommitted. The required invariant is that no committed accepted decision is excluded by a finished run with no live work. If either interleaving fails, add an evidence-owned transactional re-enqueue through `recompute_job`; rule 8 already lists it among the ledger writer's tables (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:622-625`). Do not add schedule-table writes or silently expand assessment-writer ownership.
 
 ## Build and acceptance
 
-1. Choose an evidence-owned transactional fix: stamp each newly decided chunk at DB time inside its write transaction and ensure coalescing cannot lose a decision whose commit occurs after a worker's cutoff/read, or durably enqueue an additional job for that case. Use a committed decision's DB-visible order as the invariant; simply changing the Python clock source before the transaction is insufficient. Preserve receipt times, duplicate/rejected semantics, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry.
-2. Real-Postgres race proof holds a pending/leased S3 job and a scheduled worker at its DB cutoff, then commits an overlapping late evidence chunk with a deliberately fast and slow app clock. The next tick must either include that accepted ID in the run at a cutoff at or after its decision, or find durable live work that makes the window non-CURRENT until a later run includes it. Assert the disposition time, job state, run cutoff, selection sequence and read status; no `STALE_NO_JOB` terminal gap.
-3. Crash/restart after the evidence transaction and before the next tick; replay the same batch and run ticks. Assert one accepted record, no lost job, eventual current assessment citing the ID, and no falsely CURRENT intermediate read. Check a chunk spanning two default windows, a duplicate-only chunk, and a different Site. Keep the documented alarm as defense in depth and update `backend/README-db.md` to state the remaining recovery condition accurately.

Exercise ingestion-first, worker-first and worker-skips-uncommitted-job orders. At a cutoff at or after the decision, the run must cite that ID; otherwise durable live work must keep the window non-CURRENT until a later tick includes it. No terminal `S3_STALE_NO_JOB` gap.

Manual app-clock `process-jobs` run beside the tick can still mix clocks; I3 is **not** claimed closed for manual CLIs. Retain the runbook rule to stop the tick before manual Leg B commands. Configuration `recorded_at` remains outside the closeout. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).

+## Decided clock contract
 
-## Code reality and boundary
+- Production `PostgresEvidenceLedger.decide_chunk` obtains one `clock_timestamp()` from the **database inside the chunk write transaction**, after locking the batch row and before writing its dispositions. Use this one instant for every newly decided ordinal's `ingestion_disposition.decided_at` in the chunk. Do not use `now()`/`transaction_timestamp()` (which can predate a lock wait), the ingestion host's clock, or a timestamp sampled before the transaction. The same decision instant supplies `recompute_job.available_at` and `created_at` **when `_coalesce_job` inserts a new job**. A widened PENDING job retains its existing timestamps. These writes commit or roll back together. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:176-182` currently passes the app clock; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:251-307,376-404` owns the transactional change.
+- Give the ledger an explicitly named `test_decision_clock_override` callable. When absent, always use the DB-time path; production composition at `backend/assetops_backend/main.py:265-269` must never set it. Deterministic fixtures set the override on the ledger, with one override result per chunk for both `decided_at` and a newly inserted job's `available_at`/`created_at`. The override is for fixture ordering and F-A alarm coverage, never evidence for the production race or restart proof. Change the `EvidenceLedger.decide_chunk` contract (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger.py:403-411`) so the service passes a separate app-clock `completed_at` value, never a production decision timestamp. Keep `BatchIngestionService`'s app clock for `ingestion_batch.received_at` and `completed_at` (receipt fields); a DB-stamped decision must not silently change receipt semantics.
+- Preserve duplicate/rejected outcomes, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry. Only newly processed ordinals receive a new decision instant; a retry of a committed chunk retains its stored dispositions. Configuration `recorded_at` versus `reconcile` is a separate, self-healing comparand and is outside this packet.
 
-- `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:174-184` passes `self.clock()` to each chunk before the ledger transaction. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:249-306` owns the transaction, writes accepted rows/dispositions and coalesces a recompute job; this is the writer to change.
-- `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:50-54` selects ACCEPTED dispositions only when `d.decided_at <= :k`. Scheduled stages use DB time; the cutover read and F-A reproduction are in `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:386` onward. `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204` documents the residual/alarm/requeue.
-- Rule 8 keeps the evidence ledger writer on its tables and the assessment writer on S3 jobs (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:680-757`). Keep those paths and table ownership; do not add schedule-table writes to evidence code. +## Why existing coalescing should suffice - proof obligation
+
+`_coalesce_job` locks an overlapping PENDING job with `FOR UPDATE` and widens it inside the ingestion transaction (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:376-404`). The worker lease uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:203-222`), and selection admits accepted evidence only at `d.decided_at <= :k` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:47-56`). Prove both interleavings in real Postgres: if ingestion locks/widens first, the worker cannot lease that row until ingestion commits; its later DB cutoff can see the decision. If the worker leases first, ingestion cannot widen that leased row and inserts a new PENDING job, due at the DB decision time, for the next tick. Include the blocked/rechecked status case, and prove the outcome when a tick passes while a new job is still uncommitted. The required invariant is that no committed accepted decision is excluded by a finished run with no live work. If either interleaving fails, add an evidence-owned transactional re-enqueue through `recompute_job`; rule 8 already lists it among the ledger writer's tables (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:622-625`). Do not add schedule-table writes or silently expand assessment-writer ownership.

-- `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:50-54` selects ACCEPTED dispositions only when `d.decided_at <= :k`. Scheduled stages use DB time; the cutover read and F-A reproduction are in `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:386` onward. `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204` documents the residual/alarm/requeue.
-- Rule 8 keeps the evidence ledger writer on its tables and the assessment writer on S3 jobs (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:680-757`). Keep those paths and table ownership; do not add schedule-table writes to evidence code. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).
+## Why existing coalescing should suffice - proof obligation
+
+`_coalesce_job` locks an overlapping PENDING job with `FOR UPDATE` and widens it inside the ingestion transaction (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:376-404`). The worker lease uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:203-222`), and selection admits accepted evidence only at `d.decided_at <= :k` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:47-56`). Prove both interleavings in real Postgres: if ingestion locks/widens first, the worker cannot lease that row until ingestion commits; its later DB cutoff can see the decision. If the worker leases first, ingestion cannot widen that leased row and inserts a new PENDING job, due at the DB decision time, for the next tick. Include the blocked/rechecked status case, and prove the outcome when a tick passes while a new job is still uncommitted. The required invariant is that no committed accepted decision is excluded by a finished run with no live work. If either interleaving fails, add an evidence-owned transactional re-enqueue through `recompute_job`; rule 8 already lists it among the ledger writer's tables (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:622-625`). Do not add schedule-table writes or silently expand assessment-writer ownership.
 
 ## Build and acceptance
 
-1. Choose an evidence-owned transactional fix: stamp each newly decided chunk at DB time inside its write transaction and ensure coalescing cannot lose a decision whose commit occurs after a worker's cutoff/read, or durably enqueue an additional job for that case. Use a committed decision's DB-visible order as the invariant; simply changing the Python clock source before the transaction is insufficient. Preserve receipt times, duplicate/rejected semantics, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry.
-2. Real-Postgres race proof holds a pending/leased S3 job and a scheduled worker at its DB cutoff, then commits an overlapping late evidence chunk with a deliberately fast and slow app clock. The next tick must either include that accepted ID in the run at a cutoff at or after its decision, or find durable live work that makes the window non-CURRENT until a later run includes it. Assert the disposition time, job state, run cutoff, selection sequence and read status; no `STALE_NO_JOB` terminal gap.
-3. Crash/restart after the evidence transaction and before the next tick; replay the same batch and run ticks. Assert one accepted record, no lost job, eventual current assessment citing the ID, and no falsely CURRENT intermediate read. Check a chunk spanning two default windows, a duplicate-only chunk, and a different Site. +## Decided clock contract
 
-## Code reality and boundary
+- Production `PostgresEvidenceLedger.decide_chunk` obtains one `clock_timestamp()` from the **database inside the chunk write transaction**, after locking the batch row and before writing its dispositions. Use this one instant for every newly decided ordinal's `ingestion_disposition.decided_at` in the chunk. Do not use `now()`/`transaction_timestamp()` (which can predate a lock wait), the ingestion host's clock, or a timestamp sampled before the transaction. The same decision instant supplies `recompute_job.available_at` and `created_at` **when `_coalesce_job` inserts a new job**. A widened PENDING job retains its existing timestamps. These writes commit or roll back together. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:176-182` currently passes the app clock; `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:251-307,376-404` owns the transactional change.
+- Give the ledger an explicitly named `test_decision_clock_override` callable. When absent, always use the DB-time path; production composition at `backend/assetops_backend/main.py:265-269` must never set it. Deterministic fixtures set the override on the ledger, with one override result per chunk for both `decided_at` and a newly inserted job's `available_at`/`created_at`. The override is for fixture ordering and F-A alarm coverage, never evidence for the production race or restart proof. Change the `EvidenceLedger.decide_chunk` contract (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger.py:403-411`) so the service passes a separate app-clock `completed_at` value, never a production decision timestamp. Keep `BatchIngestionService`'s app clock for `ingestion_batch.received_at` and `completed_at` (receipt fields); a DB-stamped decision must not silently change receipt semantics.
+- Preserve duplicate/rejected outcomes, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry. Only newly processed ordinals receive a new decision instant; a retry of a committed chunk retains its stored dispositions. Configuration `recorded_at` versus `reconcile` is a separate, self-healing comparand and is outside this packet.
 
-- `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/ledger_service.py:174-184` passes `self.clock()` to each chunk before the ledger transaction. `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:249-306` owns the transaction, writes accepted rows/dispositions and coalesces a recompute job; this is the writer to change.
-- `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:50-54` selects ACCEPTED dispositions only when `d.decided_at <= :k`. Scheduled stages use DB time; the cutover read and F-A reproduction are in `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:386` onward. `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204` documents the residual/alarm/requeue.
-- Rule 8 keeps the evidence ledger writer on its tables and the assessment writer on S3 jobs (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:680-757`). Keep those paths and table ownership; do not add schedule-table writes to evidence code. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).
+## Why existing coalescing should suffice - proof obligation
+
+`_coalesce_job` locks an overlapping PENDING job with `FOR UPDATE` and widens it inside the ingestion transaction (`backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_evidence_ledger.py:376-404`). The worker lease uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:203-222`), and selection admits accepted evidence only at `d.decided_at <= :k` (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/adapters/postgres_assessment_store.py:47-56`). Prove both interleavings in real Postgres: if ingestion locks/widens first, the worker cannot lease that row until ingestion commits; its later DB cutoff can see the decision. If the worker leases first, ingestion cannot widen that leased row and inserts a new PENDING job, due at the DB decision time, for the next tick. Include the blocked/rechecked status case, and prove the outcome when a tick passes while a new job is still uncommitted. The required invariant is that no committed accepted decision is excluded by a finished run with no live work. If either interleaving fails, add an evidence-owned transactional re-enqueue through `recompute_job`; rule 8 already lists it among the ledger writer's tables (`tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1:622-625`). Do not add schedule-table writes or silently expand assessment-writer ownership.
 
 ## Build and acceptance
 
-1. Choose an evidence-owned transactional fix: stamp each newly decided chunk at DB time inside its write transaction and ensure coalescing cannot lose a decision whose commit occurs after a worker's cutoff/read, or durably enqueue an additional job for that case. Use a committed decision's DB-visible order as the invariant; simply changing the Python clock source before the transaction is insufficient. Preserve receipt times, duplicate/rejected semantics, exact accepted identity, Site isolation and idempotent chunk retry.
-2. Real-Postgres race proof holds a pending/leased S3 job and a scheduled worker at its DB cutoff, then commits an overlapping late evidence chunk with a deliberately fast and slow app clock. The next tick must either include that accepted ID in the run at a cutoff at or after its decision, or find durable live work that makes the window non-CURRENT until a later run includes it. Assert the disposition time, job state, run cutoff, selection sequence and read status; no `STALE_NO_JOB` terminal gap.
-3. Crash/restart after the evidence transaction and before the next tick; replay the same batch and run ticks. Assert one accepted record, no lost job, eventual current assessment citing the ID, and no falsely CURRENT intermediate read. Check a chunk spanning two default windows, a duplicate-only chunk, and a different Site. Keep the documented alarm as defense in depth and update `backend/README-db.md` to state the remaining recovery condition accurately.

Hold an overlapping PENDING job and scheduled worker around its DB cutoff; commit a late chunk under deliberately fast and slow `BatchIngestionService` app clocks. Exercise ingestion-first, worker-first and worker-skips-uncommitted-job orders. Assert the DB-sourced disposition instant and inserted job timestamps, job lock/status/window, run cutoff, selected accepted ID sequence and read state. At a cutoff at or after the decision, the run must cite that ID; otherwise durable live work must keep the window non-CURRENT until a later tick includes it. No terminal `S3_STALE_NO_JOB` gap.

Separately re-drive the existing F-A alarm test at `backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py:385-424` **through `test_decision_clock_override`**, deliberately setting `decided_at` ahead of DB time; retain its stale/no-job alarm and requeue assertions as defense-in-depth coverage, and label it as an override-path test.
+4. Update `backend/README-db.md:128-135,204,210-212` and the I3 backlog closeout accurately. Scheduled ticks use DB time for this comparand. Manual app-clock `process-jobs` run beside the tick can still mix clocks; I3 is **not** claimed closed for manual CLIs. Retain the runbook rule to stop the tick before manual Leg B commands. Configuration `recorded_at` remains outside the closeout. No migration expected; re-chain any added migration from then-merged head (main 0013; unmerged LR-01 0014/0015; S6-01a/S4-05a may add more).
